  1025	// ===========================================================================
  1026	// B8 (G2) — the run-lock acquisition must be ATOMIC, not check-then-write.
  1027	describe('loop run — atomic run-lock acquisition (B8)', () => {
  1028	  const DIR = `${loopPaths('/repo').dir}`;
  1029	  const LOCK = `${DIR}/supervisor.lock`;
  1030	
  1031	  it('RED (B8): implementation-neutral — the competitor materializes AT the exclusive-create; wx/mkdir-first refuses, check-then-write clobbers (RED)', async () => {
  1032	    const io = makeLoopRepo({ runner: undefined });
  1033	    io.superviseChild = fakeRunner(io, [{ verdict: 'APPROVED' }, { verdict: 'APPROVED' }]);
  1034	    io.__runner = io.superviseChild;
  1035	    io.processAlive = (p) => p === 55555; // the competitor supervisor is LIVE
  1036	
  1037	    const realWrite = io.fs.writeFileSync.bind(io.fs);
  1038	    const realMkdir = io.fs.mkdirSync.bind(io.fs);
  1039	    const realRename = io.fs.renameSync.bind(io.fs);
  1040	    const COMPETITOR = JSON.stringify({ host: 'loop-host', pid: 55555, startTime: 222, runId: 'competitor' });
  1041	
  1042	    // The competitor wins the lock the INSTANT before the supervisor's own write
  1043	    // to LOCK lands — injected at whichever primitive the acquire uses to create
  1044	    // it. NO readFileSync hook, so a wx/mkdir-FIRST acquire (which never reads
  1045	    // before creating) cannot dodge the race.
  1046	    let injected = false;
  1047	    const injectBefore = (/** @type {any} */ target) => {
  1048	      if (injected || String(target) !== LOCK) return;
  1049	      injected = true;
  1050	      realMkdir(DIR, { recursive: true });
  1051	      realWrite(LOCK, COMPETITOR);
  1052	    };
  1053	
  1054	    // Exclusive create (flag wx/ax) enforces the O_EXCL semantics memfs lacks:
  1055	    // once the competitor exists, an exclusive write throws EEXIST.
  1056	    io.fs.writeFileSync = (/** @type {any} */ p, /** @type {any} */ data, /** @type {any} */ opts) => {
  1057	      const flag = typeof opts === 'string' ? undefined : opts?.flag;
  1058	      const exclusive = flag === 'wx' || flag === 'ax' || flag === 'wx+' || flag === 'ax+';
  1059	      injectBefore(p);
  1060	      if (exclusive && String(p) === LOCK && io.fs.existsSync(p)) throw Object.assign(new Error(`EEXIST: file already exists, open '${p}'`), { code: 'EEXIST' });
  1061	      return realWrite(p, data, opts); // a PLAIN write to LOCK clobbers the competitor
  1062	    };
  1063	    // mkdirSync(LOCK) as an exclusive lock dir: memfs already throws EEXIST when a
  1064	    // node exists at the path (the injected competitor file).
  1065	    io.fs.mkdirSync = (/** @type {any} */ p, /** @type {any} */ opts) => { injectBefore(p); return realMkdir(p, opts); };
  1066	    // A tmp+rename publish (atomicWriteJson) clobbers the competitor on rename.
  1067	    io.fs.renameSync = (/** @type {any} */ from, /** @type {any} */ to) => { injectBefore(to); return realRename(from, to); };
  1068	
  1069	    const code = await cmdLoop(['run'], io);
  1070	    assert.equal(code, 1, 'a wx/mkdir-first exclusive acquire observes EEXIST and refuses the run');
  1071	    assert.equal(io.__runner.calls.length, 0, 'no child spawned when the lock was lost to the competitor');
  1072	    assert.match(JSON.parse(io.files()[LOCK]).runId, /competitor/, "the competitor's lock is not clobbered (a check-then-write path fails here)");
  1073	  });
  1074	});
  1075	
  1076	// ===========================================================================
  1077	// G4 (A4) — record in-flight child groups; kill recorded orphans on reclaim.
  1078	describe('loop run — child-group recording + kill-on-reclaim (G4)', () => {
  1079	  const DIR = `${loopPaths('/repo').dir}`;
  1080	
  1081	  const readChildren = (io) => {
  1082	    const raw = io.files()[`${DIR}/children.ndjson`];
  1083	    if (typeof raw !== 'string') return [];
  1084	    return raw.split('\n').filter((l) => l.trim() !== '').map((l) => JSON.parse(l));
  1085	  };
  1086	
  1087	  it('RED (G4): a child-start record {childId, pid, pgid, startedAt} exists on disk WHILE the child is in flight (before completion)', async () => {
  1088	    const io = makeLoopRepo({ runner: undefined });
  1089	    let n = 0;
  1090	    const inflightOk = [];
  1091	    // The runner reports the child's group via opts.onStart (spawn time); the
  1092	    // supervisor must persist the record synchronously in that handler — BEFORE
  1093	    // the child completes — so a mid-run crash leaves the orphan reap-able.
  1094	    io.superviseChild = (/** @type {any} */ _spec, /** @type {any} */ opts) => {
  1095	      n += 1;
  1096	      const info = { pid: 8000 + n, pgid: 9000 + n };
  1097	      if (typeof opts.onStart === 'function') opts.onStart(info);
  1098	      const rec = readChildren(io).find((r) => r.pgid === info.pgid);
  1099	      inflightOk.push(
  1100	        !!rec && typeof rec.childId === 'string' && rec.childId.length > 0 && rec.pid === info.pid && typeof rec.startedAt === 'string' && rec.startedAt.length > 0,
  1101	      );
  1102	      return Promise.resolve({ timedOut: false, exitCode: 0, verdict: 'APPROVED', findings: '', logPath: opts.logPath, ...info });
  1103	    };
  1104	    const code = await cmdLoop(['run'], io);
  1105	    assert.equal(code, 0);
  1106	    assert.ok(inflightOk.length >= 1, 'at least one child ran');
  1107	    assert.ok(inflightOk.every(Boolean), 'each child-start record ({childId,pid,pgid,startedAt}) existed on disk while its child was in flight');
  1108	  });
  1109	
  1110	  it('RED (G5/G4): on dead-lock reclaim, a still-alive recorded orphan group is KILLED (io.processKill) BEFORE the first new spawn', async () => {
  1111	    const io = makeLoopRepo({ runner: undefined });
  1112	    const events = []; // ordered: kills and spawns interleaved
  1113	    io.processKill = (/** @type {number} */ pid, /** @type {any} */ sig) => { events.push({ kind: 'kill', pid, sig }); return true; };
  1114	    const baseRunner = fakeRunner(io, [{ verdict: 'APPROVED' }, { verdict: 'APPROVED' }]);
  1115	    io.superviseChild = (/** @type {any} */ spec, /** @type {any} */ opts) => { events.push({ kind: 'spawn' }); return baseRunner(spec, opts); };
  1116	    io.__runner = baseRunner;
  1117	    // The dead supervisor's orphan group (pgid 9001) is still alive; its lock is dead.
  1118	    io.processAlive = (/** @type {number} */ p) => p === 9001;
  1119	    io.fs.mkdirSync(DIR, { recursive: true });
  1120	    io.fs.writeFileSync(`${DIR}/supervisor.lock`, JSON.stringify({ host: 'loop-host', pid: 999999, startTime: 7, runId: 'crashed' }));
  1121	    io.fs.writeFileSync(`${DIR}/children.ndjson`, JSON.stringify({ childId: '001-plan', pid: 8001, pgid: 9001, startedAt: T0 }) + '\n');
  1122	
  1123	    const code = await cmdLoop(['run'], io);
  1124	    assert.equal(code, 0, `the reclaiming run completes after reaping orphans; stderr: ${io.stderrText()}`);
  1125	    const firstSpawn = events.findIndex((e) => e.kind === 'spawn');
  1126	    const killIdx = events.findIndex((e) => e.kind === 'kill' && Math.abs(e.pid) === 9001);
  1127	    assert.ok(killIdx >= 0, `the orphan group 9001 was killed on reclaim; events: ${JSON.stringify(events)}`);
  1128	    assert.ok(firstSpawn === -1 || killIdx < firstSpawn, 'the orphan group was killed BEFORE the first new child spawned');
  1129	  });
  1130	});
  1131	
  1132	// ===========================================================================
  1133	// G6 (B6) — `baton loop resume`: PARKED -> running; ESCALATED refused.
  1134	describe('loop run — resume (G6)', () => {
  1135	  const DIR = `${loopPaths('/repo').dir}`;
  1136	  const seedState = (over) => ({
  1137	    schema: 'baton/loop-state@1',
  1138	    runId: 'loop-r',
  1139	    goal: 'Ship the loop',
  1140	    phaseCount: 2,
  1141	    phaseIndex: 0,

  1143	    status: 'running',

  1151	    specDigest: dedupeKey(loopSpec().phases),
  1152	    ...over,
  1153	  });
  1154	
  1155	  it('RED (G6): `baton loop resume` transitions a PARKED run back to running and continues (spawns the pending phase)', async () => {

  1174

  1181	
  1182	  it('RED (G6): resume with NOTHING to resume REFUSES (exit 2) and never initializes a fresh run', async () => {
  1183	    // A loop repo with loop.json but NO persisted state.json — e.g. `resume` run
  1184	    // in the wrong directory. It must refuse, not init a fresh run from phase 0.

  1192	    const out = io.stderrText() + io.stdoutText();
  1193	    assert.match(out, /nothing to resume/i, 'the refusal states there is nothing to resume');
  1194	    assert.match(out, /baton loop run/, 'the refusal points at `baton loop run` to start a fresh run');
  1195	    assert.equal(io.__runner.calls.length, 0, 'a nothing-to-resume refusal spawns no children');
  1196	    // The teeth: a wrong-directory resume must not initialize and start over.
  1197	    assert.equal(io.fs.existsSync(`${DIR}/state.json`), false, 'refusing to resume never creates a state.json');
  1198	    assert.ok(!io.fs.existsSync(`${DIR}/supervisor.lock`), 'the nothing-to-resume refusal leaves no supervisor.lock behind (I4)');
  1199	  });
  1200	
  1201	  it('RED (G6): `baton loop run` on a PARKED run refuses (exit 4) AND points at `resume with: baton loop resume` (symmetry with pipeline)', async () => {
  1202	    const io = makeLoopRepo({ runner: undefined });

  1204	    io.__runner = io.superviseChild;
  1205	    io.fs.mkdirSync(DIR, { recursive: true });

  1207

  1209	    assert.equal(code, 4, 'a plain `run` on a parked state refuses with the parked exit code');
  1210	    const out = io.stderrText() + io.stdoutText();
  1211	    assert.match(out, /parked/i, 'the refusal says the run is parked');
  1212	    assert.match(out, /resume with: baton loop resume/, 'the refusal points at the resume command (pipeline already carries this pointer — pin the symmetry)');
  1213	    assert.equal(io.__runner.calls.length, 0, 'a `run` on a parked state spawns nothing');
  1214	    assert.equal((await loadLoopState('/repo', io)).state.status, LOOP_STATUS.PARKED, 'the run stays parked (run never resumes it)');
  1215	  });
  1216	});
  1217	
  1218	// ===========================================================================
  1219	// H3 (A2/B5) — children.ndjson RETIRES completed children; reclaim kills only
  1220	// still-in-flight groups (never an OS-recycled pid of a retired child).
  1221	describe('loop run — child registry retirement (H3)', () => {
  1222	  const DIR = `${loopPaths('/repo').dir}`;
  1223	  const readChildren = (io) => {
  1224	    const raw = io.files()[`${DIR}/children.ndjson`];
  1225	    if (typeof raw !== 'string') return [];
  1226	    return raw.split('\n').filter((l) => l.trim() !== '').map((l) => JSON.parse(l));
  1227	  };
  1228	
  1229	  it('RED (H3a): after a clean run, EVERY started child has a matching retire record ({childId, endedAt})', async () => {
  1230	    const io = makeLoopRepo({ runner: undefined });
  1231	    // A runner that reports its group (so the supervisor writes the START record
  1232	    // via its onStart handler); the RETIRE record is the supervisor's job on resolve.
  1233	    let n = 0;
  1234	    const notRetiredAtStart = [];
  1235	    io.superviseChild = (/** @type {any} */ _spec, /** @type {any} */ opts) => {
  1236	      n += 1;
  1237	      const pgid = 9000 + n;
  1238	      if (typeof opts.onStart === 'function') opts.onStart({ pid: 8000 + n, pgid });
  1239	      // Ordering: the child is IN FLIGHT here — it must have a start record and
  1240	      // NO retire yet (retirement happens only on resolution).
  1241	      const start = readChildren(io).find((r) => r.pgid === pgid);
  1242	      const retiredEarly = readChildren(io).some((r) => start && r.childId === start.childId && typeof r.endedAt === 'string');
  1243	      notRetiredAtStart.push(!!start && !retiredEarly);
  1244	      return Promise.resolve({ timedOut: false, exitCode: 0, verdict: 'APPROVED', findings: '', logPath: opts.logPath, pgid });
  1245	    };
  1246	    io.__runner = { calls: [] };
  1247	    const code = await cmdLoop(['run'], io);
  1248	    assert.ok(notRetiredAtStart.length >= 1 && notRetiredAtStart.every(Boolean), 'a just-started child has a start record and NO endedAt while in flight');
  1249	    assert.equal(code, 0);
  1250	    const recs = readChildren(io);
  1251	    const started = recs.filter((r) => typeof r.pgid === 'number').map((r) => r.childId);
  1252	    const retired = recs.filter((r) => typeof r.endedAt === 'string').map((r) => r.childId);
  1253	    assert.ok(started.length >= 1, 'at least one child started');
  1254	    for (const id of started) assert.ok(retired.includes(id), `child ${id} was retired (a {childId, endedAt} record exists)`);
  1255	  });
  1256	
  1257	  it('RED (H3b): a dead-lock reclaim KILLS NOTHING for a RETIRED child, even when processAlive says its (recycled) pgid is alive', async () => {
  1258	    const io = makeLoopRepo({ runner: undefined });
  1259	    io.superviseChild = fakeRunner(io, [{ verdict: 'APPROVED' }, { verdict: 'APPROVED' }]);
  1260	    io.__runner = io.superviseChild;
  1261	    const killCalls = [];

  1383	  it('RED (I2a): run 1 retired childId X; run 2 has a FRESH in-flight X (no retire) + dead lock -> reclaim MUST kill X', async () => {
  1384	    const io = makeLoopRepo({ runner: undefined });

  1389	    // The resumed run's in-flight child (pgid 9002) is alive; run 1's retired X is not.
  1390	    io.processAlive = (/** @type {number} */ p) => p === 9002;

  1397	        JSON.stringify({ childId: '001-plan', endedAt: T0 }) + '\n' +
  1398	        // Run 2 (the crashed resume): the SAME childId '001-plan' started again (pgid 9002), never retired.

  1400	    );
  1401	
  1402	    const code = await cmdLoop(['run'], io);
  1403	    assert.equal(code, 0, `the reclaiming run completes; stderr: ${io.stderrText()}`);
  1404	    assert.ok(killCalls.some((k) => Math.abs(k.pid) === 9002), `the resumed in-flight X (9002) is killed despite an earlier retire of the same childId; kills: ${JSON.stringify(killCalls)}`);
  1405	  });
  1406	
  1407	  it('RED (I2b): a NORMAL (non-reclaim) acquisition truncates prior-invocation records — a stale retire cannot linger to mask a future child', async () => {

  1452	// I5 (B5) — the run lock passes startTime to processAlive: a recycled pid
  1453	// (alive by pid alone but failing the pid+startTime pair) is reclaimed, not
  1454	// refused as live.
  1455	describe('loop run — lock reclaim uses pid+startTime (I5)', () => {
  1456	  const DIR = `${loopPaths('/repo').dir}`;
  1457	  it('RED (I5): a lock whose pid is alive by a 1-arg check but fails the (pid, startTime) pair is treated as DEAD and reclaimed', async () => {
  1458	    const io = makeLoopRepo({ runner: undefined });
  1459	    io.superviseChild = fakeRunner(io, [{ verdict: 'APPROVED' }, { verdict: 'APPROVED' }]);
  1460	    io.__runner = io.superviseChild;
  1461	    // pid 7777 reads as alive for ANY defined startTime EXCEPT the recorded 999
  1462	    // (which is the reused-pid case → dead). A 1-arg check, or a wrong-but-defined
  1463	    // second arg, would read live and wedge the lock. Only passing the RECORDED
  1464	    // startTime (999) reclaims — so the fake is inverted vs. a "match one value"
  1465	    // trap, and the calls are recorded to prove the pair is passed through.
  1466	    const aliveCalls = [];
  1467	    io.processAlive = (/** @type {number} */ pid, /** @type {number | undefined} */ startTime) => {
  1468	      aliveCalls.push({ pid, startTime });
  1469	      if (pid !== 7777) return false;
  1470	      return startTime !== 999; // dead ONLY for the recorded (pid, startTime) pair
  1471	    };
  1472	    io.fs.mkdirSync(DIR, { recursive: true });
  1473	    io.fs.writeFileSync(`${DIR}/supervisor.lock`, JSON.stringify({ host: 'loop-host', pid: 7777, startTime: 999, runId: 'recycled' }));
  1474	
  1475	    const code = await cmdLoop(['run'], io);
  1476	    assert.equal(code, 0, `a reused-pid lock is reclaimed (not refused as live); stderr: ${io.stderrText()}`);
  1477	    assert.ok(io.__runner.calls.length >= 1, 'the run proceeds after reclaiming the reused-pid lock');
  1478	    assert.ok(
  1479	      aliveCalls.some((c) => c.pid === 7777 && c.startTime === 999),
  1480	      `the reclaim passes the RECORDED (pid, startTime) pair to processAlive; calls: ${JSON.stringify(aliveCalls)}`,
  1481	    );
  1482	  });
  1483	});

+// ===========================================================================
+// ITEM 10 (v1.1) — --detach. `baton loop run --detach` forks a background
+// supervisor that survives the invoking terminal; the parent returns promptly
+// once the detached child owns the run lock, printing its pid + a tail hint.
+//
+// SEAM JUDGMENT CALL (flagged for the verifier): a REAL fork is not honestly
+// unit-testable (an orphaned process, timing), so these pin the behavior through
+// an injected `io.spawnDetached(spec) -> { pid }` seam the implementation should
+// honor (default: a real detached child_process.spawn). Assumptions pinned:
+//   - the PARENT checks the run lock (refuse-live / reclaim-dead) BEFORE forking;
+//   - the DETACHED CHILD owns the lock (the fake writes it naming the child pid);
+//   - spawnDetached receives the capped supervisor.out output path.
+// Windows: --detach is POSIX-only (documented unsupported) — SKIP_WIN.
+describe('loop run — --detach (item 10)', () => {
+  const SKIP_WIN = process.platform === 'win32';
+  const DIR = loopPaths('/repo').dir;
+  const detachSeam = (io, pid, { writeLock = true } = {}) => {
+    const rec = { calls: [] };
+    io.spawnDetached = (/** @type {any} */ spec) => {
+      rec.calls.push(spec);
+      if (writeLock) {
+        io.fs.mkdirSync(DIR, { recursive: true });
+        io.fs.writeFileSync(`${DIR}/supervisor.lock`, JSON.stringify({ host: io.host, pid, startTime: 1, runId: `sup-${pid}` }));
+      }
+      return { pid };
+    };
+    return rec;
+  };
+
+  it('RED (10-1): --detach forks a detached supervisor; the parent exits 0 after the child owns the lock, printing pid + tail hint', { skip: SKIP_WIN }, async () => {
+    const io = makeLoopRepo({ runner: undefined });
+    const DETACHED_PID = 424242;
+    const seam = detachSeam(io, DETACHED_PID);
+    const code = await cmdLoop(['run', '--detach'], io);
+    assert.equal(code, 0, `the parent returns promptly with exit 0; stderr: ${io.stderrText()}`);
+    assert.equal(seam.calls.length, 1, 'the supervisor was forked via the detach seam exactly once');
+    const lock = JSON.parse(io.files()[`${DIR}/supervisor.lock`]);
+    assert.equal(lock.pid, DETACHED_PID, 'the run lock names the detached supervisor pid');
+    assert.match(io.stdoutText(), new RegExp(String(DETACHED_PID)), 'stdout prints the detached pid');
+    assert.match(io.stdoutText(), /supervisor\.out/, 'stdout prints a tail hint for .handoff/loop/supervisor.out');
+  });
+
+  it('RED (10-2): --detach refuses (exit 1) when a LIVE supervisor already holds the lock — no fork', { skip: SKIP_WIN }, async () => {
+    const io = makeLoopRepo({ runner: undefined });
+    io.processAlive = (/** @type {number} */ pid) => pid === 55555;
+    const seam = detachSeam(io, 1);
+    io.fs.mkdirSync(DIR, { recursive: true });
+    io.fs.writeFileSync(`${DIR}/supervisor.lock`, JSON.stringify({ host: 'loop-host', pid: 55555, startTime: 222, runId: 'live' }));
+    const code = await cmdLoop(['run', '--detach'], io);
+    assert.equal(code, 1, 'a live lock refuses the detached run (exit 1)');
+    assert.equal(seam.calls.length, 0, 'no detached supervisor is forked while the lock is held live');
+  });
+
+  it('RED (10-3): --detach reclaims a DEAD lock, killing recorded in-flight child groups BEFORE forking', { skip: SKIP_WIN }, async () => {
+    const io = makeLoopRepo({ runner: undefined });
+    const kills = /** @type {any[]} */ ([]);
+    io.processKill = (/** @type {number} */ pid, /** @type {any} */ sig) => { kills.push({ pid, sig }); return true; };
+    io.processAlive = (/** @type {number} */ pid) => pid === 9001; // orphan group alive; lock pid 999999 dead
+    const seam = detachSeam(io, 7);
+    io.fs.mkdirSync(DIR, { recursive: true });
+    io.fs.writeFileSync(`${DIR}/supervisor.lock`, JSON.stringify({ host: 'loop-host', pid: 999999, startTime: 7, runId: 'crashed' }));
+    io.fs.writeFileSync(`${DIR}/children.ndjson`, JSON.stringify({ childId: '001-plan', pid: 8001, pgid: 9001, startedAt: T0 }) + '\n');
+    const code = await cmdLoop(['run', '--detach'], io);
+    assert.equal(code, 0, `the reclaiming detached run starts; stderr: ${io.stderrText()}`);
+    assert.ok(kills.some((k) => k.pid === -9001), `the orphan group 9001 was killed on reclaim; kills: ${JSON.stringify(kills)}`);
+    assert.equal(seam.calls.length, 1, 'after reclaiming, the detached supervisor is forked');
+  });
+
+  it('GUARD (10-4): a completing run releases the lock — the release contract a detached supervisor reuses', { skip: SKIP_WIN }, async () => {
+    // The detached child runs the same supervisor body; isolating its release
+    // needs a real child, so this pins the reused contract on the attached path.
+    const io = makeLoopRepo({ spec: loopSpec({ phases: [{ id: 'work', role: 'implementer' }] }), runner: undefined });
+    io.superviseChild = fakeRunner(io, [{ verdict: 'APPROVED' }]);
+    io.__runner = io.superviseChild;
+    const code = await cmdLoop(['run'], io);
+    assert.equal(code, 0, `the run completes; stderr: ${io.stderrText()}`);
+    assert.ok(!io.fs.existsSync(`${DIR}/supervisor.lock`), 'a completing run releases the supervisor.lock');
+  });
+
+  it('RED (10-5): --detach directs the detached output to a byte-capped .handoff/loop/supervisor.out', { skip: SKIP_WIN }, async () => {
+    const io = makeLoopRepo({ runner: undefined });
+    const seam = detachSeam(io, 7);
+    await cmdLoop(['run', '--detach'], io);
+    assert.equal(seam.calls.length, 1, 'the detach seam was invoked');
+    const spec = seam.calls[0];
+    assert.match(String(spec.outPath ?? spec.out ?? ''), /\.handoff\/loop\/supervisor\.out$/, 'the detached output is directed to .handoff/loop/supervisor.out');
+    const cap = spec.maxBytes ?? spec.cap ?? spec.capBytes;
+    assert.ok(typeof cap === 'number' && cap > 0, `a positive byte cap is passed for supervisor.out; got ${JSON.stringify(cap)}`);
+  });
+});

+// ===========================================================================
+// ITEM 9 (v1.1) — pipeline smoke gate. After the FINAL subtask merges, a
+// pipeline whose loop.json sets smoke.cmd reuses the loop's smoke machinery
+// (smokeApprovalToken/verifySmokeToken, SMOKE_AWAIT/SMOKE_APPROVE, --approve-smoke):
+// run smoke.cmd, await a drift-bound token, complete only on a verified token.
+describe('pipeline — smoke gate (item 9)', () => {
+  const DIR = loopPaths('/repo').dir;
+  // A git that also answers the smoke command (non-git → 'ok'), so merges work
+  // AND the smoke.cmd runs through io.execFile.
+  const smokeAwareIo = (spec, { git = pipelineGit() } = {}) => {
+    const io = makePipeRepo({ spec, git, runner: undefined });
+    const baseExec = io.execFile;
+    io.execFile = (/** @type {string} */ cmd, /** @type {string[]} */ args = [], /** @type {any} */ opts = {}) =>
+      cmd === 'git' ? baseExec(cmd, args, opts) : Promise.resolve({ stdout: 'ok\n', stderr: '' });
+    io.superviseChild = fakeRunner(io, cleanSubtask());
+    io.__runner = io.superviseChild;
+    return io;
+  };
+  const smokeSpec = () => pipelineSpec({ subtasks: [{ id: 't1', title: 'only' }], smoke: { cmd: 'npm run smoke', expect: 'ok' } });
+
+  it('RED (9-1): a pipeline with smoke.cmd AWAITS approval after the last subtask merges (not straight to DONE)', async () => {
+    const io = smokeAwareIo(smokeSpec());
+    const code = await run(['pipeline', 'run'], io);
+    assert.equal(code, 0, `awaiting smoke approval is a clean exit 0; stderr: ${io.stderrText()}`);
+    assert.equal((await loadLoopState('/repo', io)).state.status, LOOP_STATUS.AWAITING_SMOKE_APPROVAL, 'the run awaits smoke approval, not DONE');
+    assert.ok(io.files()[`${DIR}/merges.ndjson`]?.includes('t1'), 'the subtask merged BEFORE the smoke gate');
+    assert.ok(io.files()[`${DIR}/SMOKE-REVIEW.md`], 'SMOKE-REVIEW.md was written');
+    const approval = io.files()[`${DIR}/smoke-approval.json`];
+    assert.ok(approval, 'smoke-approval.json was written');
+    const token = JSON.parse(approval).token;
+    assert.ok(typeof token === 'string' && token.startsWith('smk1.'), 'a drift-bound smk token is issued');
+  });
+
+  it('RED (9-2): pipeline run --approve-smoke <token> verifies and completes the run DONE', async () => {
+    const io = smokeAwareIo(smokeSpec());
+    assert.equal(await run(['pipeline', 'run'], io), 0);
+    const approval = io.files()[`${DIR}/smoke-approval.json`];
+    assert.ok(approval, 'run 1 reached the smoke gate and issued a token');
+    const token = JSON.parse(approval).token;
+
+    const code = await run(['pipeline', 'run', '--approve-smoke', token], io);
+    assert.equal(code, 0, `an approved pipeline completes; stderr: ${io.stderrText()}`);
+    const raw = JSON.parse(io.files()[loopPaths('/repo').state]);
+    assert.equal(raw.status, 'done', 'the approved run persists status done');
+  });
+
+  it('RED (9-3): a DRIFTED smoke token is refused and the pipeline PARKS (exit 4)', async () => {
+    const git = pipelineGit();
+    const io = smokeAwareIo(smokeSpec(), { git });
+    assert.equal(await run(['pipeline', 'run'], io), 0);
+    const approval = io.files()[`${DIR}/smoke-approval.json`];
+    assert.ok(approval, 'run 1 issued a token');
+    const token = JSON.parse(approval).token;
+    // A bound input drifts: main moves after the human looked.
+    git.moveMain('DRIFTED-SHA-9-3-AAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAA');
+    const code = await run(['pipeline', 'run', '--approve-smoke', token], io);
+    assert.equal(code, 4, 'a drifted token parks the pipeline (exit 4)');
+    assert.equal((await loadLoopState('/repo', io)).state.status, LOOP_STATUS.PARKED, 'drift parks the run');
+  });
+
+  it('GUARD (9-4): a pipeline with NO smoke.cmd completes straight to DONE (no regression)', async () => {
+    const io = makePipeRepo({ spec: pipelineSpec({ subtasks: [{ id: 't1', title: 'only' }] }), runner: undefined });
+    io.superviseChild = fakeRunner(io, cleanSubtask());
+    io.__runner = io.superviseChild;
+    const code = await run(['pipeline', 'run'], io);
+    assert.equal(code, 0);
+    assert.equal((await loadLoopState('/repo', io)).state.status, LOOP_STATUS.DONE, 'no smoke.cmd → straight to DONE');
+    assert.ok(!io.files()[`${DIR}/smoke-approval.json`], 'no smoke gate is armed when smoke.cmd is unset');
+  });
+
+  it('GUARD (9-5): a pipeline spec with smoke.cmd and NO phases is ACCEPTED — the loop smoke-build rule never gates pipelines', async () => {
+    const spec = smokeSpec();
+    assert.ok(!('phases' in spec), 'precondition: the pipeline spec has subtasks, no phases');
+    const io = smokeAwareIo(spec);
+    const code = await run(['pipeline', 'run'], io);
+    assert.notEqual(code, 2, 'smoke.cmd + no phases is NOT a validation error for a pipeline (no smoke-build refusal)');
+    assert.ok(io.__runner.calls.length >= 1, 'it ran past validation and spawned the subtask');
+  });
+});

+describe('pipeline — --detach (item 10)', () => {

+    io.spawnDetached = (/** @type {any} */ spec) => {

+      io.fs.mkdirSync(DIR, { recursive: true });
+      io.fs.writeFileSync(`${DIR}/supervisor.lock`, JSON.stringify({ host: io.host, pid: DETACHED_PID, startTime: 1, runId: `sup-${DETACHED_PID}` }));
+      return { pid: DETACHED_PID };

   746	// ===========================================================================
   747	// ITEM 10 (v1.1) — --detach. `baton loop run --detach` forks a background
   748	// supervisor that survives the invoking terminal; the parent returns promptly
   749	// once the detached child owns the run lock, printing its pid + a tail hint.
   750	//
   751	// SEAM JUDGMENT CALL (flagged for the verifier): a REAL fork is not honestly
   752	// unit-testable (an orphaned process, timing), so these pin the behavior through
   753	// an injected `io.spawnDetached(spec) -> { pid }` seam the implementation should
   754	// honor (default: a real detached child_process.spawn). Assumptions pinned:
   755	//   - the PARENT checks the run lock (refuse-live / reclaim-dead) BEFORE forking;
   756	//   - the DETACHED CHILD owns the lock (the fake writes it naming the child pid);
   757	//   - spawnDetached receives the capped supervisor.out output path.
   758	// Windows: --detach is POSIX-only (documented unsupported) — SKIP_WIN.
   759	describe('loop run — --detach (item 10)', () => {
   760	  const SKIP_WIN = process.platform === 'win32';
   761	  const DIR = loopPaths('/repo').dir;
   762	  const detachSeam = (io, pid, { writeLock = true } = {}) => {
   763	    const rec = { calls: [] };
   764	    io.spawnDetached = (/** @type {any} */ spec) => {
   765	      rec.calls.push(spec);
   766	      if (writeLock) {
   767	        io.fs.mkdirSync(DIR, { recursive: true });
   768	        io.fs.writeFileSync(`${DIR}/supervisor.lock`, JSON.stringify({ host: io.host, pid, startTime: 1, runId: `sup-${pid}` }));
   769	      }
   770	      return { pid };
   771	    };
   772	    return rec;
   773	  };
   774	
   775	  it('RED (10-1): --detach forks a detached supervisor; the parent exits 0 after the child owns the lock, printing pid + tail hint', { skip: SKIP_WIN }, async () => {
   776	    const io = makeLoopRepo({ runner: undefined });
   777	    const DETACHED_PID = 424242;
   778	    const seam = detachSeam(io, DETACHED_PID);
   779	    const code = await cmdLoop(['run', '--detach'], io);
   780	    assert.equal(code, 0, `the parent returns promptly with exit 0; stderr: ${io.stderrText()}`);
   781	    assert.equal(seam.calls.length, 1, 'the supervisor was forked via the detach seam exactly once');
   782	    const lock = JSON.parse(io.files()[`${DIR}/supervisor.lock`]);
   783	    assert.equal(lock.pid, DETACHED_PID, 'the run lock names the detached supervisor pid');
   784	    assert.match(io.stdoutText(), new RegExp(String(DETACHED_PID)), 'stdout prints the detached pid');
   785	    assert.match(io.stdoutText(), /supervisor\.out/, 'stdout prints a tail hint for .handoff/loop/supervisor.out');
   786	  });
   787	
   788	  it('RED (10-2): --detach refuses (exit 1) when a LIVE supervisor already holds the lock — no fork', { skip: SKIP_WIN }, async () => {

   281	  function smokeExecFile(rec) {
   282	    return (/** @type {string} */ cmd, /** @type {string[]} */ args = []) => {
   283	      rec.push([cmd, ...args].join(' '));
   284	      // git stays unavailable; the smoke command "passes".
   285	      if (cmd === 'git') return Promise.reject(Object.assign(new Error('spawn ENOENT'), { code: 'ENOENT' }));
   286	      return Promise.resolve({ stdout: 'ok\n', stderr: '' });
   287	    };
   288	  }

33:export function dedupeKey(obj) {
     1	import { createHash } from 'node:crypto';
     2	
     3	/**
     4	 * New bundle id from an injectable random source (deterministic in tests).
     5	 * @param {() => number} [rand]
     6	 */
     7	export function newBundleId(rand = Math.random) {
     8	  const a = rand().toString(36).slice(2);
     9	  const b = rand().toString(36).slice(2);
    10	  return `b_${(a + b).slice(0, 16) || '0'}`;
    11	}
    12	
    13	/**
    14	 * Canonical JSON: object keys sorted ascending at every depth; array order
    15	 * preserved (order is meaningful there).
    16	 * @param {any} value @returns {string}
    17	 */
    18	function canonicalJson(value) {
    19	  if (Array.isArray(value)) {
    20	    return `[${value.map(canonicalJson).join(',')}]`;
    21	  }
    22	  if (value !== null && typeof value === 'object') {
    23	    const keys = Object.keys(value).sort();
    24	    return `{${keys.map((k) => `${JSON.stringify(k)}:${canonicalJson(value[k])}`).join(',')}}`;
    25	  }
    26	  return JSON.stringify(value);
    27	}
    28	
    29	/**
    30	 * sha256 hex over canonical JSON — the journal's idempotency key.
    31	 * @param {unknown} obj @returns {string}
    32	 */
    33	export function dedupeKey(obj) {
    34	  return createHash('sha256').update(canonicalJson(obj)).digest('hex');
    35	}

     3	 * mirroring the bundle store's crash-tolerance idioms (atomic tmp+rename
     4	 * snapshots, torn-tail-tolerant NDJSON, seq-dedup replay). Everything lives
     5	 * under <root>/.handoff/loop/; the supervisor is the sole writer. Plan:
     6	 * docs/plans/2026-07-18-goal-loop-worktree-pipeline.md §"baton loop",
     7	 * §"Smoke gate", §"Supervisor lifetime and recovery", §"Caps".
     8	 */
     9	import { appendEntry, readAllTolerant } from '../util/jsonl.mjs';
    10	import { atomicWriteJson, safeReadJson, ensureDir } from '../util/fsx.mjs';
    11	import { dedupeKey } from '../util/ids.mjs';
    12	
    13	export const LOOP_STATE_SCHEMA = 'baton/loop-state@1';
    14	
    15	/** The frozen status vocabulary. */
    16	export const LOOP_STATUS = Object.freeze({
    17	  RUNNING: 'running',
    18	  AWAITING_SMOKE_APPROVAL: 'awaiting-smoke-approval',
    19	  PARKED: 'parked',
    20	  ESCALATED: 'escalated',
    21	  DONE: 'done',
    22	});
    23	
    24	/** The frozen journal event vocabulary. */
    25	export const LOOP_EVENT = Object.freeze({
    26	  PHASE_ADVANCE: 'phase-advance',
    27	  GATE_ITERATION: 'gate-iteration',
    28	  ESCALATE: 'escalate',
    29	  PARK: 'park',
    30	  RESUME: 'resume',
    31	  SMOKE_AWAIT: 'smoke-await',
    32	  SMOKE_APPROVE: 'smoke-approve',
    33	});
    34	
    35	// Every gate loops at most 5 times — a repo-wide hard invariant (AGENTS.md
    36	// §Review gates); the reducer refuses a 6th and escalates instead.
    37	const GATE_ITERATION_CAP = 5;
    38	
    39	/** @param {string} root */
    40	export function loopPaths(root) {
    41	  const dir = `${root}/.handoff/loop`;
    42	  return { dir, state: `${dir}/state.json`, journal: `${dir}/journal.ndjson` };
    43	}
    44	
    45	/**
    46	 * Fresh run state — pure and deterministic from the injected io (identity
    47	 * facts + clock only; no Date.now/Math.random, which would break replay).
    48	 * @param {any} spec a VALIDATED loop spec @param {any} io
    49	 */
    50	export function initLoopState(spec, io) {
    51	  const runId = `loop-${dedupeKey({ host: io.host, pid: io.pid, startTime: io.startTime, at: io.now() }).slice(0, 12)}`;
    52	  /** @type {Record<string, number>} */
    53	  const iterations = {};
    54	  for (const p of spec.phases ?? []) {
    55	    if (/gate/.test(String(p.id))) iterations[p.id] = 0;
    56	  }
    57	  return {
    58	    schema: LOOP_STATE_SCHEMA,
    59	    runId,
    60	    goal: spec.goal,
    61	    phaseCount: Array.isArray(spec.phases) ? spec.phases.length : 0,
    62	    phaseIndex: 0,
    63	    iterations,
    64	    status: LOOP_STATUS.RUNNING,
    65	    parkReason: null,
    66	    escalation: null,
    67	    smokeApproval: null,
    68	    createdAt: io.now(),
    69	    journalSeq: 0,
    70	  };
    71	}
    72	
    73	/**
    74	 * The pure transition reducer. Never mutates its input; unknown events and
    75	 * refused transitions return the input state unchanged (a new reference is
    76	 * only produced for a real transition).
    77	 * @param {any} state @param {any} ev
    78	 */
    79	export function applyLoopEvent(state, ev) {
    80	  switch (ev?.type) {
    81	    case LOOP_EVENT.PHASE_ADVANCE: {
    82	      const next = state.phaseIndex + 1;
    83	      if (next >= state.phaseCount) return { ...state, phaseIndex: state.phaseCount, status: LOOP_STATUS.DONE };
    84	      return { ...state, phaseIndex: next };
    85	    }
    86	    case LOOP_EVENT.GATE_ITERATION: {
    87	      const gate = String(ev.gate);
    88	      const current = state.iterations?.[gate] ?? 0;
    89	      // The 6th attempt is REFUSED: the counter stays at the cap and the run
    90	      // escalates with a record naming the gate — never a 6th run.
    91	      if (current >= GATE_ITERATION_CAP) {
    92	        return { ...state, status: LOOP_STATUS.ESCALATED, escalation: { gate, iteration: current } };
    93	      }
    94	      return { ...state, iterations: { ...state.iterations, [gate]: current + 1 } };
    95	    }
    96	    case LOOP_EVENT.ESCALATE: {
    97	      // Supervisors enforce spec caps LOWER than the hard invariant; this
    98	      // event persists that escalation (dogfood finding D7: a cap-3 pipeline
    99	      // exit left state.json 'running' because only the reducer's own 5-cap
   100	      // could flip the status). Idempotent — an already-escalated run keeps
   101	      // its original record.
   102	      if (state.status === LOOP_STATUS.ESCALATED) return state;
   103	      const gate = String(ev.gate);
   104	      return { ...state, status: LOOP_STATUS.ESCALATED, escalation: { gate, iteration: state.iterations?.[gate] ?? 0 } };
   105	    }
   106	    case LOOP_EVENT.PARK:
   107	      return { ...state, status: LOOP_STATUS.PARKED, parkReason: ev.reason ?? null };
   108	    case LOOP_EVENT.RESUME:
   109	      // Only a PARK is resumable; an escalation is an operator decision.
   110	      if (state.status !== LOOP_STATUS.PARKED) return state;
   111	      return { ...state, status: LOOP_STATUS.RUNNING, parkReason: null };
   112	    case LOOP_EVENT.SMOKE_AWAIT:
   113	      return { ...state, status: LOOP_STATUS.AWAITING_SMOKE_APPROVAL };
   114	    case LOOP_EVENT.SMOKE_APPROVE: {
   115	      // Guarded transition: only meaningful while awaiting, and only with the
   116	      // caller's verifySmokeToken result stamped verified === true. A missing
   117	      // or false `verified` is unverified — it never resumes the run.
   118	      if (state.status !== LOOP_STATUS.AWAITING_SMOKE_APPROVAL) return state;
   119	      if (ev.verified !== true) return state;
   120	      // A smoke gate on the FINAL phase already advanced past phaseCount —
   121	      // approval completes the run as DONE, not RUNNING (dogfood finding D9:
   122	      // the printed done/exit 0 left a 'running' artifact behind).
   123	      const resumedStatus = state.phaseIndex >= state.phaseCount ? LOOP_STATUS.DONE : LOOP_STATUS.RUNNING;
   124	      return { ...state, status: resumedStatus, smokeApproval: { token: ev.token, at: ev.ts ?? null } };
   125	    }
   126	    default:
   127	      return state;
   128	  }
   129	}

   133	 * @param {string} root @param {any} state @param {any} io
   134	 */
   135	export async function writeLoopState(root, state, io) {

 succeeded in 0ms:
   119	 * child groups (.handoff/loop/children.ndjson) are KILLED first so no
   120	 * orphan survives the takeover, then the lock is retried once.
   121	 * Shared by `loop run` and `pipeline run`.
   122	 * @param {string} root @param {any} io @param {string} cmdLabel
   123	 * @returns {Promise<{ok: true, release: () => void} | {ok: false, code: number}>}
   124	 */
   125	export async function acquireSupervisorLock(root, io, cmdLabel) {
   126	  const p = loopPaths(root);
   127	  const lockPath = `${p.dir}/supervisor.lock`;
   128	  const childrenPath = `${p.dir}/children.ndjson`;
   129	  ensureDir(io.fs, p.dir);
   130	  const payload = JSON.stringify({ host: io.host, pid: io.pid, startTime: io.startTime, runId: `sup-${io.pid}` });
   131	
   132	  const tryExclusive = () => {
   133	    // Fast-path an already-present lock (fakes without O_EXCL semantics rely
   134	    // on this); the wx flag below is the REAL exclusivity on a POSIX fs — a
   135	    // competitor that lands between the check and the write throws EEXIST
   136	    // instead of being clobbered.
   137	    if (io.fs.existsSync(lockPath)) return false;
   138	    try {
   139	      io.fs.writeFileSync(lockPath, payload, { flag: 'wx' });
   140	      return true;
   141	    } catch (err) {
   142	      if (/** @type {any} */ (err)?.code === 'EEXIST') return false;
   143	      throw err;
   144	    }
   145	  };
   146	
   147	  for (let attempt = 1; attempt <= 2; attempt += 1) {
   148	    if (tryExclusive()) {
   149	      // A fresh acquisition owns a fresh child registry: prior invocations'
   150	      // start/retire records are truncated so a stale retire can never mask a
   151	      // later in-flight child that reuses the same childId (I2).
   152	      try {
   153	        if (io.fs.existsSync(childrenPath)) io.fs.unlinkSync(childrenPath);
   154	      } catch {
   155	        // Best effort.
   156	      }
   157	      return {
   158	        ok: true,
   159	        release: () => {
   160	          try {
   161	            if (io.fs.existsSync(lockPath)) io.fs.unlinkSync(lockPath);
   162	          } catch {
   163	            // Best effort — a stale lock is recoverable via the dead path.
   164	          }
   165	        },
   166	      };
   167	    }
   168	    const existing = safeReadJson(io.fs, lockPath);
   169	    const other = existing.ok ? existing.value : null;
   170	    // Liveness verifies the (pid, startTime) PAIR — a recycled pid without the
   171	    // recorded start time is a dead owner, not a live supervisor (I5).
   172	    const alive = other && typeof other.pid === 'number' && typeof io.processAlive === 'function' ? io.processAlive(other.pid, other.startTime) : true;
   173	    const sameProcess = other && other.pid === io.pid && other.startTime === io.startTime;
   174	    if (!sameProcess && (!other || other.host !== io.host || alive)) {
   175	      io.stderr.write(`baton ${cmdLabel}: another supervisor (pid ${other?.pid ?? 'unknown'} on ${other?.host ?? 'unknown'}, run ${other?.runId ?? '?'}) holds the run lock — refusing a second supervisor\n`);
   176	      return { ok: false, code: 1 };
   177	    }
   178	    // Provably dead (or our own stale lock): reap its recorded in-flight
   179	    // child groups BEFORE anything else runs, then reclaim.
   180	    if (io.fs.existsSync(childrenPath)) {
   181	      const lines = String(io.fs.readFileSync(childrenPath, 'utf8')).split('\n').filter((l) => l.trim() !== '');
   182	      // Match retires to starts IN ORDER per childId — a resumed invocation
   183	      // reuses childIds, and a prior run's retire must never mask the newer
   184	      // in-flight start of the same id (I2). Whatever start remains unmatched
   185	      // is genuinely in-flight.
   186	      /** @type {Map<string, any[]>} */
   187	      const inflight = new Map();
   188	      for (const line of lines) {
   189	        try {
   190	          const rec = JSON.parse(line);
   191	          if (typeof rec?.endedAt === 'string') inflight.get(rec.childId)?.shift();
   192	          else if (typeof rec?.pgid === 'number') {
   193	            if (!inflight.has(rec.childId)) inflight.set(rec.childId, []);
   194	            inflight.get(rec.childId)?.push(rec);
   195	          }
   196	        } catch {
   197	          // A torn record is unreapable — skip it.
   198	        }
   199	      }
   200	      // Kill ONLY genuinely in-flight children (start with no matching
   201	      // retire): a retired child's pgid may have been recycled by an
   202	      // unrelated live process — killing it would be a pid-reuse casualty (H3).
   203	      for (const recs of inflight.values()) {
   204	        for (const rec of recs) {
   205	          if (typeof io.processAlive === 'function' && io.processAlive(rec.pgid)) {
   206	            if (typeof io.processKill === 'function') io.processKill(-rec.pgid, 'SIGKILL');
   207	          }
   208	        }
   209	      }
   210	      try {
   211	        io.fs.unlinkSync(childrenPath);
   212	      } catch {
   213	        // Best effort.
   214	      }
   215	    }
   216	    io.stderr.write(`baton ${cmdLabel}: reclaimed the run lock from a provably-dead supervisor (pid ${other?.pid})\n`);
   217	    try {
   218	      io.fs.unlinkSync(lockPath);
   219	    } catch {
   220	      // Already gone.
   221	    }
   222	  }
   223	  io.stderr.write(`baton ${cmdLabel}: could not acquire the run lock after reclaiming — another supervisor keeps winning\n`);
   224	  return { ok: false, code: 1 };
   225	}

   218	const SMOKE_TOKEN_PREFIX = 'smk1';
   219	const SMOKE_TOKEN_FIELDS = /** @type {const} */ ([
   220	  'stateDigest',
   221	  'smokeCmd',
   222	  'smokeOutputDigest',
   223	  'gitHead',
   224	  'gitContentDigest',
   225	  'childAssignment',
   226	]);
   227	
   228	/**
   229	 * Deterministic structured token. Per-field digests keep field boundaries
   230	 * (no concatenation collisions) and let verification name drifted inputs.
   231	 * Missing bound fields throw — a token over partial inputs is meaningless.
   232	 * @param {Record<string, string>} inputs
   233	 * @returns {string}
   234	 */
   235	export function smokeApprovalToken(inputs) {
   236	  for (const f of SMOKE_TOKEN_FIELDS) {
   237	    if (typeof inputs?.[f] !== 'string') throw new Error(`smoke approval token: required input '${f}' is missing`);
   238	  }
   239	  return `${SMOKE_TOKEN_PREFIX}.` + SMOKE_TOKEN_FIELDS.map((f) => dedupeKey(inputs[f]).slice(0, 10)).join('.');
   240	}
   241	
   242	/**
   243	 * Recompute against current inputs; positional comparison names every
   244	 * drifted input. Never throws — partial current inputs simply cannot verify.
   245	 * @param {string} presented @param {Record<string, string>} inputs
   246	 * @returns {{ok: true} | {ok: false, driftedInputs: string[]}}
   247	 */
   248	export function verifySmokeToken(presented, inputs) {
   249	  /** @type {string} */
   250	  let current;
   251	  try {
   252	    current = smokeApprovalToken(inputs);
   253	  } catch {
   254	    return { ok: false, driftedInputs: SMOKE_TOKEN_FIELDS.filter((f) => typeof inputs?.[f] !== 'string') };
   255	  }
   256	  if (presented === current) return { ok: true };
   257	  const a = String(presented ?? '').split('.');
   258	  const b = current.split('.');
   259	  /** @type {string[]} */
   260	  const drifted = [];
   261	  SMOKE_TOKEN_FIELDS.forEach((f, i) => {
   262	    if (a[i + 1] !== b[i + 1]) drifted.push(f);
   263	  });
   264	  return { ok: false, driftedInputs: drifted.length > 0 ? drifted : [...SMOKE_TOKEN_FIELDS] };
   265	}

   294	  const transition = async (/** @type {any} */ ev) => {

   297	    state = { ...next, journalSeq: seq };
   298	    await writeLoopState(root, state, io);
   299	    return state;
   300	  };

   305	  // cache reads null — offline-degraded, exactly as before.

    43	/**
    44	 * @param {string[]} args @param {any} io
    45	 * @returns {Promise<number>}

    47	export async function cmdPipeline(args, io) {
    48	  /** @type {string[]} */
    49	  const positionals = [];
    50	  /** @type {string[]} */
    51	  const flagTokens = [];
    52	  for (let i = 0; i < args.length; i += 1) {
    53	    const a = args[i];
    54	    if (typeof a === 'string' && a.startsWith('--')) {
    55	      flagTokens.push(a);
    56	      if (STRING_FLAGS.has(a.slice(2)) && i + 1 < args.length) {
    57	        i += 1;
    58	        flagTokens.push(args[i]);
    59	      }
    60	    } else {
    61	      positionals.push(a);
    62	    }
    63	  }
    64	  const parsed = parseFlagsStrict(flagTokens, {});
    65	  if (parsed.error !== undefined) return usageError(io, parsed.flags, 'pipeline', parsed.error);
    66	  const flags = parsed.flags;
    67	
    68	  const sub = positionals[0];
    69	  if (sub === undefined) return usageError(io, flags, 'pipeline', 'a subcommand is required — try: baton pipeline run');
    70	  if (sub !== 'run' && sub !== 'resume') return usageError(io, flags, 'pipeline', `unknown subcommand '${sub}' (supported: run, resume)`);
    71	  if (positionals.length > 1) return usageError(io, flags, 'pipeline', `unexpected argument '${positionals[1]}'`);
    72	  if (sub === 'resume') flags.__resume = true;
    73	
    74	  return runPipeline(flags, io);
    75	}

    77	/**
    78	 * Derive the repo's trunk branch EXACTLY ONCE per run: origin's HEAD when it
    79	 * exists, else the currently checked-out branch at the root. The result is
    80	 * stamped into state and reused on every resume — never re-derived mid-run.
    81	 * @param {string} root @param {any} io
    82	 * @returns {Promise<string>}
    83	 */
    84	async function deriveTrunk(root, io) {
    85	  try {
    86	    const out = String((await io.execFile('git', ['symbolic-ref', 'refs/remotes/origin/HEAD'], { cwd: root })).stdout).trim();
    87	    const m = out.match(/refs\/remotes\/origin\/(.+)$/);
    88	    if (m?.[1]) return m[1];
    89	  } catch {
    90	    // No origin HEAD (local-only repo) — fall back to the checked-out branch.
    91	  }
    92	  const branch = String((await io.execFile('git', ['rev-parse', '--abbrev-ref', 'HEAD'], { cwd: root })).stdout).trim();
    93	  return branch.length > 0 && branch !== 'HEAD' ? branch : 'main';
    94	}
    95	
    96	/** Validate the spec's subtasks — every rejection names its offender.
    97	 * @param {any} subtasks @returns {string | null} */
    98	function validateSubtasks(subtasks) {
    99	  if (!Array.isArray(subtasks) || subtasks.length === 0) {
   100	    return 'loop.json needs a non-empty `subtasks` array ([{id, title}]) for a pipeline run';
   101	  }
   102	  const seen = new Set();
   103	  for (let i = 0; i < subtasks.length; i += 1) {
   104	    const st = subtasks[i];
   105	    if (typeof st?.id !== 'string' || st.id.length === 0) return `subtasks[${i}] is missing a string id`;
   106	    if (typeof st?.title !== 'string' || st.title.length === 0) return `subtasks[${i}] ('${st.id}') is missing a string title`;
   107	    if (seen.has(st.id)) return `subtasks carry a duplicate id '${st.id}' — ids name branches and must be unique`;
   108	    seen.add(st.id);
   109	  }
   110	  return null;
   111	}

 succeeded in 0ms:
     1	// Fake `io` object matching the injected-io contract from docs/design/core.md:
     2	// { cwd, env, stdin, stdout, stderr, fs, execFile, now }. Backed by memfs.
     3	// stdout/stderr collect writes; io.stdoutText()/io.stderrText() flatten them.
     4	// now() returns a settable ISO string (io.setNow). execFile resolves from an
     5	// execResults map keyed by the joined command string.
     6	//
     7	// Wave-B2 additions (bundle lock layer) — all optional, defaulted, and purely
     8	// additive so no prior consumer is affected:
     9	//   host / pid / startTime  — the process-identity facts the lock model writes
    10	//     into owner.json and compares on contention (see plan §Concurrency, Lock
    11	//     model iterations 3+4).
    12	//   processAlive(pid, startTime) — the "provably dead" oracle. Default: only
    13	//     THIS io's own {pid, startTime} is alive; every other descriptor reads as
    14	//     dead. Override per test to model a live foreign owner or a pid/start-time
    15	//     mismatch (reused pid).
    16	//   newFencingToken() — the injected per-acquisition token source. Deterministic
    17	//     monotonic counter ("tok-1", "tok-2", …), matching the injected-io
    18	//     discipline already used for now()/execFile so lock acquisition is
    19	//     reproducible in tests rather than drawing from Math.random.
    20	
    21	import { makeMemfs } from './memfs.mjs';
    22	
    23	export function makeIo({
    24	  files = {},
    25	  env = {},
    26	  stdin = '',
    27	  execResults = {},
    28	  now = '2026-07-11T00:00:00.000Z',
    29	  host = 'host-A',
    30	  pid = 4242,
    31	  startTime = 111000,
    32	  processAlive = null,
    33	  fencingSeed = 1,
    34	  execPath = process.execPath,
    35	} = {}) {
    36	  const { fs, files: filesSnapshot } = makeMemfs(files);
    37	  const outChunks = [];
    38	  const errChunks = [];
    39	  let currentNow = now;
    40	  let tokenCounter = fencingSeed - 1;
    41	  const defaultAlive = (p, s) => p === pid && s === startTime;
    42	
    43	  const stdout = { write: (s) => { outChunks.push(String(s)); return true; } };
    44	  const stderr = { write: (s) => { errChunks.push(String(s)); return true; } };
    45	
    46	  const execFile = (cmd, args = [], /* opts */ _opts = {}) => {
    47	    const key = [cmd, ...(Array.isArray(args) ? args : [])].join(' ');
    48	    return new Promise((resolve, reject) => {
    49	      if (!(key in execResults)) {
    50	        const e = new Error(`spawn ${cmd} ENOENT`);
    51	        e.code = 'ENOENT';
    52	        return reject(e);
    53	      }
    54	      const r = execResults[key];
    55	      const code = r.code ?? 0;
    56	      const out = r.stdout ?? '';
    57	      const err = r.stderr ?? '';
    58	      if (code !== 0) {
    59	        // util.promisify(execFile) rejects on nonzero exit; error carries code + streams.
    60	        const e = new Error(`Command failed: ${key}`);
    61	        e.code = code;
    62	        e.stdout = out;
    63	        e.stderr = err;
    64	        return reject(e);
    65	      }
    66	      resolve({ stdout: out, stderr: err });
    67	    });
    68	  };
    69	
    70	  return {
    71	    cwd: '/repo',
    72	    env,
    73	    stdin,
    74	    stdout,
    75	    stderr,
    76	    fs,
    77	    execFile,
    78	    now: () => currentNow,
    79	    setNow: (iso) => { currentNow = iso; },
    80	    execPath,
    81	    host,
    82	    pid,
    83	    startTime,
    84	    processAlive: processAlive || defaultAlive,
    85	    // Tiny retry budget: unit tests exercising live-lock refusal stay fast
    86	    // while the retry code path itself still runs (production default: 120x25ms).
    87	    lockRetry: { attempts: 2, delayMs: 2 },
    88	    newFencingToken: () => `tok-${++tokenCounter}`,
    89	    stdoutText: () => outChunks.join(''),
    90	    stderrText: () => errChunks.join(''),
    91	    files: filesSnapshot,
    92	  };
    93	}

   138	
   139	### Supervisor lifetime and recovery (Gate-1 iteration 2, finding 5)
   140	
   141	"Survives the operator's session ending" is a defined mechanism, not a hope:
   142	
   143	- `baton loop run` is FOREGROUND by default. `--detach` re-launches the
   144	  supervisor in its own session (`setsid`), output to
   145	  `.handoff/loop/supervisor.log`, and returns.
   146	- A run lock `.handoff/loop/supervisor.lock` carries {pid, process
   147	  start-time, host, runId} — the same provably-dead machinery as the
   148	  Layer-1 bundle lock. A second `loop run` against a LIVE supervisor is
   149	  refused; against a provably-dead one it recovers.
   150	- The state machine is journal-replayable: recovery replays
   151	  `journal.ndjson`, marks any in-flight child of the dead supervisor as
   152	  interrupted (its process group is gone or killed on adoption), and either
   153	  continues from the last completed step or parks with an escalation note —
   154	  never re-runs a completed gate iteration.
   155	- Acceptance adds a supervisor-death test: kill the supervisor (and its
   156	  launching session) mid-subtask → a fresh `loop run` recovers via the

     6	
     7	Plain ESM JavaScript (`.mjs`) with JSDoc types, checked by `tsc --noEmit --checkJs` (dev-only). No build step: hooks invoke source directly. Zero runtime dependencies; strict spec-based flag parsing (`parseFlagsStrict` in commands/shared.mjs — unknown flags, stray positionals, and missing string-flag values are exit-2 usage errors, so a garbled safety flag can never silently lose intent); `node:test` with a small golden-file helper (`UPDATE_GOLDEN=1 npm test` regenerates).    163	 * Spawn and supervise one child: detached in its OWN process group, stdin
   164	 * closed; on timeout SIGTERM the group, wait graceMs, SIGKILL the group (no
   165	 * zombie grandchildren). Output streams to a capped log at opts.logPath; the
   166	 * verdict is parsed from the full transcript, region-bounded.
   167	 * @param {{command: string, args: string[], env?: Record<string, string>, cwd?: string}} spec
   168	 * @param {{timeoutMs: number, graceMs: number, logPath: string, maxLogBytes?: number, platform?: string, fs?: {writeFileSync: Function, mkdirSync: Function}, onStart?: (info: {pid: number, pgid: number}) => void}} opts
   169	 * @returns {Promise<{timedOut: boolean, exitCode: number | null, verdict?: string, findings?: string, reason?: string, logPath: string, pgid?: number}>}
   170	 */
   171	export function superviseChild(spec, opts) {
   172	  return new Promise((resolve) => {
   173	    const maxLogBytes = opts.maxLogBytes ?? 1_000_000;
   174	    const child = spawn(spec.command, spec.args, {
   175	      cwd: spec.cwd,
   176	      env: { ...process.env, ...(spec.env ?? {}) },
   177	      stdio: ['ignore', 'pipe', 'pipe'],
   178	      detached: true, // its own process group — the kill unit
   179	    });
   180	
   181	    // Report the group at spawn time so the supervisor can persist it BEFORE
   182	    // the child completes — a supervisor crash leaves the orphan reap-able.
   183	    const pgid = typeof child.pid === 'number' ? child.pid : -1;
   184	    if (typeof opts.onStart === 'function' && typeof child.pid === 'number') {
   185	      opts.onStart({ pid: child.pid, pgid });
   186	    }

**Verified Facts**

I ran the requested targeted command suite: 128 pass / 8 red, matching the intended red count in these files. The eight reds are the new item 9 and 10 cases at [pipeline-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:1344), [pipeline-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:1357), [pipeline-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:1370), [pipeline-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:1412), [loop-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/loop-run.test.mjs:775), [loop-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/loop-run.test.mjs:788), [loop-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/loop-run.test.mjs:799), and [loop-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/loop-run.test.mjs:825). `npm run typecheck` is green.

**Seam Judgments**

1. `io.spawnDetached(spec) -> { pid }` is a sound unit seam for this boundary, consistent with the repo’s injected-io testing model in [core.md](/Users/saksham/baton/docs/design/core.md:9). But 10-5 currently under-defines the spec shape by accepting aliases for output path and cap at [loop-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/loop-run.test.mjs:831).

2. Parent precheck before fork and child-owned lock is the right contract. Plan acceptance requires parent return after the detached supervisor owns the lock in [2026-07-20-v1.1-hardening.md](/Users/saksham/baton/docs/plans/2026-07-20-v1.1-hardening.md:84), and the test comments state that assumption at [loop-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/loop-run.test.mjs:755). 10-2 does assert no fork on live lock at [loop-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/loop-run.test.mjs:796).

3. 10-4 is acceptable as a command-level guard, but not as final coverage for detached completion. The plan asks for lock release on detached completion at [2026-07-20-v1.1-hardening.md](/Users/saksham/baton/docs/plans/2026-07-20-v1.1-hardening.md:88), while the test explicitly pins only the attached path at [loop-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/loop-run.test.mjs:814).

4. Command tests with memfs plus seam are acceptable for parent command behavior in this sandbox. They are not enough to prove the POSIX/default spawn behavior required by [2026-07-20-v1.1-hardening.md](/Users/saksham/baton/docs/plans/2026-07-20-v1.1-hardening.md:83).

VERDICT: BLOCKED
FINDINGS:
1. [high] — Item 9 can pass with a pipeline smoke implementation that does not actually run `smoke.cmd` after the final subtask, and can also pass an implementation that accepts any token when inputs have not drifted. The plan requires running smoke after the final merge and completing only on a verified token at [2026-07-20-v1.1-hardening.md](/Users/saksham/baton/docs/plans/2026-07-20-v1.1-hardening.md:72). The pipeline smoke fixture has one subtask at [pipeline-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:1342), records no non-git smoke exec call at [pipeline-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:1333), 9-1 only checks receipt/files/token prefix at [pipeline-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:1348), 9-2 uses the issued token at [pipeline-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:1362), and 9-3 only checks HEAD drift at [pipeline-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:1377). — Fix: use a two-subtask smoke fixture, record `io.execFile` events, assert the smoke command runs only after the final merge receipt, assert the issued token equals `smokeApprovalToken(record.inputs)`, and add an unchanged-input bogus-token refusal case.
2. [high] — 10-3 says child pgids are killed before forking, but the test does not assert ordering. It records kills at [loop-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/loop-run.test.mjs:801), creates the detach seam at [loop-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/loop-run.test.mjs:804), then separately asserts a kill and one fork at [loop-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/loop-run.test.mjs:810). The older attached reclaim test has the needed ordered event pattern at [loop-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/loop-run.test.mjs:1112). — Fix: push ordered `{kind:'kill'}` and `{kind:'fork'}` events from `processKill` and `spawnDetached`, then assert the `-9001` kill index is before the fork index.
3. [medium] — Item 10 final POSIX/default behavior remains unpinned: detached completion release, real/default spawn, actual capped `supervisor.out`, and pipeline live/dead/cap parity can be wrong while these command tests pass. The plan requires capped output and detached lifecycle coverage at [2026-07-20-v1.1-hardening.md](/Users/saksham/baton/docs/plans/2026-07-20-v1.1-hardening.md:80), but 10-4 is attached-only at [loop-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/loop-run.test.mjs:814), 10-5 only checks seam fields at [loop-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/loop-run.test.mjs:825), and pipeline has only the basic 10-1 case at [pipeline-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/pipeline-run.test.mjs:1412). — Fix: add a POSIX real-fs integration or adapter-level default-spawn test for child-mode completion release and capped output, and add pipeline live-lock/dead-reclaim/output-cap assertions unless both commands are forced through one tested shared detach helper.
4. [low] — The defined detach seam shape is under-specified because 10-5 accepts `outPath` or `out` and `maxBytes` or `cap` or `capBytes` at [loop-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/loop-run.test.mjs:831). — Fix: choose exact seam fields, preferably `outPath` and `maxBytes`, and assert those exact fields.

**Seam Judgments**

1. `io.spawnDetached(spec) -> { pid }` is a sound unit seam for this boundary, consistent with the repo’s injected-io testing model in [core.md](/Users/saksham/baton/docs/design/core.md:9). But 10-5 currently under-defines the spec shape by accepting aliases for output path and cap at [loop-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/loop-run.test.mjs:831).

2. Parent precheck before fork and child-owned lock is the right contract. Plan acceptance requires parent return after the detached supervisor owns the lock in [2026-07-20-v1.1-hardening.md](/Users/saksham/baton/docs/plans/2026-07-20-v1.1-hardening.md:84), and the test comments state that assumption at [loop-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/loop-run.test.mjs:755). 10-2 does assert no fork on live lock at [loop-run.test.mjs](/Users/saksham/baton/tests/commands/loop-run.test.mjs:796).

4. Command tests with memfs plus seam are acceptable for parent command behavior in this sandbox. They are not enough to prove the POSIX/default spawn behavior required by [2026-07-20-v1.1-hardening.md](/Users/saksham/baton/docs/plans/2026-07-20-v1.1-hardening.md:83).
